# Add 63 reviewed retro projects across home computers, consoles, PCs, operating systems, applications and development tools.

Add 63 reviewed retro projects across home computers, consoles, PCs, operating systems, applications and development tools.

Date: 2026-10-18 · Review: ready

Snapshot check: upstream heads were not checked; pinned evidence is a historical snapshot.

### Identity (reviewed)

Commented Z80 reconstruction of TRS-80 Model I/III Level II BASIC ROMs, with enhanced variants and EACA System-80 support. The author explicitly identifies the material as a derivative disassembly rather than original Microsoft/Tandy source.
- https://github.com/kiwisincebirth/TRS-80-ROMS/blob/736bcba36c606cb766eb8932977198c7d22889f7/README.md
- https://github.com/kiwisincebirth/TRS-80-ROMS/blob/736bcba36c606cb766eb8932977198c7d22889f7/LEVEL2BASIC-012D.Z80
- https://github.com/kiwisincebirth/TRS-80-ROMS/blob/736bcba36c606cb766eb8932977198c7d22889f7/MDL1LEV2.Z80

### Classification (reviewed)

Implementation has named error/graphics routines, hardware symbols, address assertions and documented overlapping-instruction tricks. README claims comparisons with official ROMs, not independently verified. No standalone license file in pinned tree. README Legal preserves original authors’ rights and claims no ownership; source availability is not a redistribution grant.

### Identity (reviewed)

Preservation release of Ed Fries’ Romox Atari 8-bit game, with original source disk and Kay Savetz’s exported text files. Kept as one source-release family.
- https://github.com/savetz/edfries-frog/blob/6b6df593de664d1cb73012e56a0372f2541ac125/README.md

- https://github.com/savetz/edfries-frog/commit/6b6df593de664d1cb73012e56a0372f2541ac125

### Classification (reviewed)

Inspected DLI routine saving/restoring registers, programming WSYNC and colors, plus relocation loader. Source disk is explicitly authoritative; text exports are not assumed complete modern rebuild inputs. MIT LICENSE explicitly names Ed Fries (2021); README says he released this game source under MIT.

### Identity (reviewed)

Reusable C cross-assembler implementing Mac/65-compatible syntax, macros, Atari directives, binary/image output and emulator-state injection. Selected maintained Peter Hinz fork as the single ATasm lineage record. 
- https://github.com/CycoPH/atasm/blob/46be895a4312205dbcc4cf7a4a6721d72219d711/README.md
- https://github.com/CycoPH/atasm/blob/46be895a4312205dbcc4cf7a4a6721d72219d711/src/asm.c

- https://github.com/CycoPH/atasm/commit/46be895a4312205dbcc4cf7a4a6721d72219d711

### Classification (reviewed)

Large real assembler core implements expression/macro processing, multiple passes, symbols and object generation; tests cover syntax and historical crash cases. Pinned v1.30 full commit message has no AI trailers. GPL-2.0 license file. Review indicates this is maintained derivative work, not original OSS Mac/65 code.

### Identity (reviewed)

Expanded maintained Python collection for TRS-80 Color Computer software development. Its substantial DECB-to-BASIC09 translator is beyond the small image-conversion ancestor; retained as one CoCo Tools family record. 
- https://github.com/jamieleecho/coco-tools/blob/5eebd9968c278c0749b7ab5b7278bf62967d0a9e/README.md
- https://github.com/jamieleecho/coco-tools/blob/5eebd9968c278c0749b7ab5b7278bf62967d0a9e/README.decb-to-b09.md
- https://github.com/jamieleecho/coco-tools/blob/5eebd9968c278c0749b7ab5b7278bf62967d0a9e/coco/b09/compiler.py
- https://github.com/jamieleecho/coco-tools/blob/5eebd9968c278c0749b7ab5b7278bf62967d0a9e/coco/decb_to_b09.py
- https://github.com/jamieleecho/coco-tools/blob/5eebd9968c278c0749b7ab5b7278bf62967d0a9e/pyproject.toml
- https://github.com/jamieleecho/coco-tools/blob/5eebd9968c278c0749b7ab5b7278bf62967d0a9e/LICENSE
- https://github.com/jamieleecho/coco-tools/commit/5eebd9968c278c0749b7ab5b7278bf62967d0a9e

### Classification (reviewed)

Actual grammar/visitor compiler parses Color BASIC, transforms control flow, variables, arrays and numeric representations, and emits BASIC09. Separate modules convert CoCo image formats. Extensive tests and examples are present. GPL-2.0 license file and package declaration; historical contributed converters retain attribution.
- https://github.com/jamieleecho/coco-tools/blob/5eebd9968c278c0749b7ab5b7278bf62967d0a9e/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

C/assembly development framework with MSX hardware backend, SDL host backend, Konami-5 mapper linker and font/MIDI conversion utilities. It lets the same application sources be tested on Linux then compiled to MSX cartridges.
- https://github.com/MartinezTorres/sdcc_msx/blob/0948ce6584bb99b3d3187be2bc8c5e6f5d93fefb/README.md
- https://github.com/MartinezTorres/sdcc_msx/blob/0948ce6584bb99b3d3187be2bc8c5e6f5d93fefb/Makefile.in
- https://github.com/MartinezTorres/sdcc_msx/blob/0948ce6584bb99b3d3187be2bc8c5e6f5d93fefb/src/msxhal_msx.c
- https://github.com/MartinezTorres/sdcc_msx/blob/0948ce6584bb99b3d3187be2bc8c5e6f5d93fefb/src/gif_v01.c
- https://github.com/MartinezTorres/sdcc_msx/blob/0948ce6584bb99b3d3187be2bc8c5e6f5d93fefb/util/k5link.cc
- https://github.com/MartinezTorres/sdcc_msx/blob/0948ce6584bb99b3d3187be2bc8c5e6f5d93fefb/LICENSE
- https://github.com/MartinezTorres/sdcc_msx/commit/0948ce6584bb99b3d3187be2bc8c5e6f5d93fefb

### Classification (reviewed)

Reviewed platform HAL, graphics routines and substantial custom K5 linker. Restriction to Konami-5 MegaROM and manually declared module page mapping is documented. Modified MIT: in addition to MIT terms, commercial products must provide one free copy to author. Do not label unmodified MIT.
- https://github.com/MartinezTorres/sdcc_msx/blob/0948ce6584bb99b3d3187be2bc8c5e6f5d93fefb/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Configurable C/Z80 game-development runtime covering MSX graphics, input, audio, storage and memory banking, plus build and asset tools. Many sample games/demos are examples within one reusable library project.

Reviewed actual VDP register/mode management implementation with inline Z80/interrupt handling, source headers, build wrapper and JS orchestration. Preserve mixed-license qualification.

### Identity (reviewed)

Portable C game library with target-specific graphics/input/sound and a coordinated game collection including Cross Chase, Shoot, Bomber, Snake and Horde. Canonical upstream supersedes the stale miguelqvd/CROSS-CHASE fork discovered first.

### Classification (reviewed)

Reviewed concrete display implementation (direct video memory paths and platform graphics operations), Chase game loop/state and level generation, and target/compiler make rules. Broad target status is documented but not independently tested. Some selected Chase make rules reference generated/combined main.c that is absent in the pinned directory; split source files are present, so build completeness is unverified. Custom non-commercial, zlib-like license: origin attribution, altered-source marking and notice preservation; explicitly not standard zlib/OSI unrestricted licensing.

Treat the documented build path as unverified, not build-ready. No candidate software was compiled or executed; build/run/play/byte-identity flags remain unknown.

### Identity (reviewed)

An independently written ARM implementation of the Amiga Batman game with converted graphics, scrolling tile maps, compiled sprites, player movement/collision tests and projectile updates. This is a partial game port, not the already tracked MarkyTurtle Amiga source reconstruction.
- https://github.com/scottjmoore/batman-archimedes/blob/b670ceb5f74c5af53f374919609e1903c3fe8307/src/batman.asm
- https://github.com/scottjmoore/batman-archimedes/blob/b670ceb5f74c5af53f374919609e1903c3fe8307/src/sprites.asm
- https://github.com/scottjmoore/batman-archimedes/blob/b670ceb5f74c5af53f374919609e1903c3fe8307/makefile
- https://github.com/scottjmoore/batman-archimedes/blob/b670ceb5f74c5af53f374919609e1903c3fe8307/README.md
- https://github.com/scottjmoore/batman-archimedes/commit/b670ceb5f74c5af53f374919609e1903c3fe8307

### Classification (reviewed)

Approximately 1,600 lines in the main ARM file plus hardware and sprite modules. Main loop has movement, collision and bullets, but visible TODOs and debug controls mean completeness must not be inferred. No repository-wide licence found. Main assembly states Scott Moore 2021, all rights reserved; Batman and ripped game artwork retain third-party rights.
- https://github.com/scottjmoore/batman-archimedes/blob/b670ceb5f74c5af53f374919609e1903c3fe8307/src/batman.asm

### Identity (reviewed)

A native C++ side-by-side text-file difference viewer with an Amiga GUI, Myers-derived diff engine, selection/search/navigation and background worker code.
- https://github.com/rosneru/ADiffView/blob/64548c6ae302114e03beeb62c387d10eba4e9891/src/diffengine/DiffEngine.cpp

### Identity (reviewed)

Darren Coles’s substantial Amiga E rewrite of the historical AmiExpress BBS, authorised to use the name by the former rights holder. Includes the node/controller, setup/user tools, file-transfer protocols, mail, FTP and HTTP services.
- https://github.com/dmcoles/AmiExpress/blob/d5beb95e8ca58ec5195fa5e4315f118dfd26035d/express.e

Daniel Stenberg and Kjell Ericson’s historical Amiga editor sources, continued by Vidar Hokstad, with editing/undo, text folding, scriptable FPL integration, ARexx and syntax-sensitive display.

### Identity (reviewed)

Niklas Lindholm’s Swedish KOM-model Amiga BBS, originally developed 1990–1996, later maintained by Tomas Kärki, and revived from NiKom 1.60 sources in 2015. Contains node/server code, conferences, file areas, Fido support and utility libraries.
- https://github.com/punktniklas/NiKom/blob/537b13289e7c21ae715521b71a82360f18b7e495/Nodes/KOM.c

Hynek Schlawack and Sebastian Bauer’s open-source MUI mail client with POP3, IMAP, SMTP, MIME handling, configuration, address book and Amiga-family platform backends.

### Identity (reviewed)

Long-running Amiga-family MIME mail user agent, begun by Marcel Beck in 1995 and continued by the YAM team, with MUI GUI, POP3/SMTP transport, folders, identities, filters, encryption integration and ARexx.
- https://github.com/jens-maus/yam/blob/0ba6556668cdc745a0fb121c3226b3afcf5432e3/src/YAM.c

Oxyron’s Amiga OCS demo source plus reusable trackmo framework: floppy track loader, Doynamite-derived 68k depacker, P61 player, memory manager, effect launcher and many individual demo parts, with host asset tools.

### Identity (reviewed)

Chris Bazley’s native RISC OS relocatable module implements joystick SWIs using keypad input, with switched, analogue and damped modes, chiefly to test game joystick support without hardware.

### Identity (reviewed)

Three native Amiga commands read floppy tracks into ADF images, write ADF images to real drives and copy disks with verification, backed by shared track-device I/O code.
- https://github.com/alpine9000/adf-tools/blob/9e1a6c0e895e3f15621853c7004bc4bc3769a074/adfwrite.c

Native Game & Watch-style Lynx game with two game modes, segmented number sprites, score, rabbit position, cocotte timer, lives and action-speed state. ingame.c is substantive implementation and generated assembly is also retained.

### Identity (reviewed)

Full native tile-matching RPG/puzzle game released in 2014. Assembly contains horizontal/vertical matching, board solvability checks, combat, animation, sound and link multiplayer, with art and auxiliary checksum tool.

### Identity (reviewed)

Native Game Boy game prototype inspired by the football section of Tiny Toons Adventures: Buster Busts Loose. Includes fixed-point object acceleration, active-object bitfields, animation, sprites, joypad and smooth parallax/background implementation.

### Identity (reviewed)

Real-time native GBC lighting shader demo. Assembly computes simplified dot products with log-space cosine/power lookup tables, run-length encoded normal data and packed Game Boy tile output; Python prepares normal-map sequences.

### Identity (reviewed)

Very early native Hyper Neo Geo 64 SDK with MIPS startup/vector setup, TLB/core initialization, linker/build rules and an implemented Go ELF-to-cartridge ROM packer. The empty sample is only an example of the reusable SDK, not a separate game.

### Identity (reviewed)

Native controller test application that counts presses of a selected button over ten seconds. Actual C state machine reads BIOS current/previous input, detects rising edges, tracks VBlank time and displays totals.
- https://github.com/sleepy-danny/ngrapidfire-tester/blob/8e3ff355c2146aee2efc8cf75e325e74f406f4b3/rapidfire-tester/rftester.c

### Identity (reviewed)

Charles Petzold’s September 1990 Microsoft Systems Journal checkers game, preserved with its original sources and a 2026 Open Watcom 32-bit PM continuation. The implementation includes board/judge windows, legal-move generation, recursive jump enumeration and a threaded computer strategy.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-BOARD-Checkers/blob/af8b322abc3967253de223ab524384415815d690/README.md
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-BOARD-Checkers/blob/af8b322abc3967253de223ab524384415815d690/src/ckrstrat.c
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-BOARD-Checkers/blob/af8b322abc3967253de223ab524384415815d690/src/ckrjudge.c

- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-BOARD-Checkers/commit/af8b322abc3967253de223ab524384415815d690

### Classification (reviewed)

Original 1990 OS/2 PM C sources and separate legacy tree; six-language UI, key remapping and INI settings added. GPLv3 stated in README and accompanying license text; original magazine source is retained with attribution. This is a source-availability review, not independent verification of the relicensing chain.

### Identity (reviewed)

Todd B. Crowe’s native OS/2 vector-style Asteroids clone, updated for Open Watcom and ArcaOS. This is the OS/2 game’s source continuation, distinct from the Atari arcade disassembly and unrelated Asteroids homebrews already catalogued. The implementation has game modes, two-player scoring, motion/drawing helpers and configurable keyboard/mouse/joystick input.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-ACTION-Asteroids/blob/ddac03de3538dc70b1fc73cc7fdf42d095b41ce6/README.md
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-ACTION-Asteroids/blob/ddac03de3538dc70b1fc73cc7fdf42d095b41ce6/src/ASTEROID.C
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-ACTION-Asteroids/blob/ddac03de3538dc70b1fc73cc7fdf42d095b41ce6/doc/License.txt

- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-ACTION-Asteroids/commit/ddac03de3538dc70b1fc73cc7fdf42d095b41ce6

### Classification (reviewed)

32-bit PM game plus a 16-bit private-font DLL and six IPF help files. The font resource generator is external to the repo, although generated ASTEROID.RES is checked in. README specifies GPLv3-or-later and the GPLv3 text is included. Original-author attribution remains.

### Identity (reviewed)

Restoration and 32-bit OS/2 PM continuation of Microsoft’s 1988 OS/2 Toolkit JIGSAW sample. It slices a BMP into 36 draggable pieces and preserves GPI retained-segment transforms, piece/island manipulation, zooming and asynchronous drawing. The original 16-bit source is retained in legacy; this is unrelated to the similarly named Jigsaw electronic-puzzle game already indexed.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-PUZZLE-JigSaw/blob/1bce50ced27159e7e6e002d4526a3a8569f7127d/README.md
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-PUZZLE-JigSaw/blob/1bce50ced27159e7e6e002d4526a3a8569f7127d/src/jigsaw.c
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-PUZZLE-JigSaw/blob/1bce50ced27159e7e6e002d4526a3a8569f7127d/doc/LICENSE.txt

- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-PUZZLE-JigSaw/blob/1bce50ced27159e7e6e002d4526a3a8569f7127d/legacy/JIGSAW.C
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-PUZZLE-JigSaw/commit/1bce50ced27159e7e6e002d4526a3a8569f7127d

### Classification (reviewed)

Native 32-bit PM continuation rather than a new game-specific emulation wrapper. README links the older PM-Jigsaw sample repository as lineage, not an additional candidate. Port/enhancements use BSD-3-Clause; the license explicitly says the original 1988 Microsoft sample’s license is not specified. Do not describe the entire codebase as uniformly BSD.

### Identity (reviewed)

Frank Sven Nestel’s 1995–2001 PM arithmetic-puzzle game and associated generator/evaluator/solver sources, updated in 2026 for Open Watcom. Actual source implements several legal equation families, randomized puzzle generation, evaluation and a threaded Presentation Manager interface.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-EDUCATIONAL-PM_Equator/blob/5cb2b856dfaa8d7a754edbdbde7b00cf66ba776f/README.md

### Classification (reviewed)

Includes console generator/solver tools plus EQUATOR.EXE, six-language help and resource bitmaps. Source headers explicitly permit GPLv2-or-later and point to the included GPLv2 HTML text. README says GPLv2; legacy/LICENSE contains GPLv3. A superseded shareware notice is explicitly named ignore.old.license.txt; preserve these qualifications.

### Identity (reviewed)

Charles Petzold’s magazine-published 1989 OS/2 15-puzzle code, continued as a 32-bit PM application. Source handles row/column tile slides, blank tracking, scramble/reset, solved-state detection and the elapsed timer, with 2026 localization and settings additions.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-PUZZLE-Jeu_de_Taquin/blob/10f00f36efd3f202e12b97564fa27417cadc085a/README.md
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-PUZZLE-Jeu_de_Taquin/blob/10f00f36efd3f202e12b97564fa27417cadc085a/src/taquin.c
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-PUZZLE-Jeu_de_Taquin/blob/10f00f36efd3f202e12b97564fa27417cadc085a/doc/LICENSE.txt

- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-PUZZLE-Jeu_de_Taquin/commit/10f00f36efd3f202e12b97564fa27417cadc085a

### Classification (reviewed)

Distinct OS/2 source lineage; do not confuse generic sliding-puzzle implementations with this Petzold source. README/doc/LICENSE.txt describe original Ziff Communications educational example code and public-domain/educational-use treatment for 2026 modifications. Separate doc/LICENSE is BSD-3-Clause. Original educational publication is not a clean blanket public-domain grant; rights remain qualified.

### Identity (reviewed)

David Boudah/Bou Software Systems’ 1996 IBM Open Class Boggle game, with original C++ preserved under legacy and a 2026 plain-C PM rewrite. Board source implements Fisher-Yates dice shuffling and Qu handling; dictionary and UI modules validate words and run the timed game without the original IBM C++ class libraries.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-EDUCATIONAL-Boggle/blob/68eee213f7396482fcd4216070b31cf492fe49f5/README.md
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-EDUCATIONAL-Boggle/blob/68eee213f7396482fcd4216070b31cf492fe49f5/src/BOARD.C

- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-EDUCATIONAL-Boggle/commit/68eee213f7396482fcd4216070b31cf492fe49f5

Six-language UI does not imply six-language dictionaries: word checking remains English. Original C++ and rewritten C are one lineage, not separate projects. GPLv2 stated in README and included full GPLv2 text.

### Identity (reviewed)

Enumerate Inc.’s 1996–1999 polygon-stopping game, preserving its original V GUI/X11/Windows95 C++ sources and a 2026 OS/2 SDL2 front end. Polygon and walk classes maintain vertices and motion; the new main loop handles input, menus, timed bonuses, lives and drawing.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-STRATEGY-ZeroPoint/blob/db08dfd1d357245a044fe78f15a4443babafb1b9/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Rene Auberger’s 1999 native OS/2 Mastermind game, with its legacy C source and a 2026 Open Watcom continuation that consolidates the language/resource DLL design into one executable. Source renders guess rows, drag/drop pins, exact/color-only feedback, timers and high scores.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-STRATEGY-MasterMind2/blob/de6c8bfa1ded7b8645883bcdc2d26d637a86a9db/README.md

Seven interface languages and per-language help outputs. README notes six cursor resources must be extracted from the original global.dll, which is retained under OLD; asset extraction was not performed. README says Eclipse Public License; doc/LICENSE is EPL-2.0. Retained old sources are attributed to Rene Auberger.

### Identity (reviewed)

Mike of Vienna’s 1998 OS/2 bird-shooting game, preserved with original C and an Open Watcom/ArcaOS continuation. The PM message procedure moves bird sprites, performs mouse hit tests, counts kills, supports the all-birds action and manages pause/focus/timer state.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-OTHERS-Birdman/blob/1e4ae3bab062ab84e90893f4b7b2b0754a53c4a8/README.md
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-OTHERS-Birdman/blob/1e4ae3bab062ab84e90893f4b7b2b0754a53c4a8/src/birdman.c

- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-OTHERS-Birdman/commit/1e4ae3bab062ab84e90893f4b7b2b0754a53c4a8

### Classification (reviewed)

Original and updated source trees, sprite/pointer resources and 2026 build system are present; six-language menus and persistence added. BSD-3-Clause license text in both root LICENSE and doc/LICENSE.txt; source attribution credits the original 1998 author.

### Identity (reviewed)

E. L. Zapanta’s IBM-era 1992–1994 native 32-bit OS/2 bingo game, preserved with original C and a 2026 Open Watcom build. Source contains randomized card/number creation, multiple bingo-pattern checks, score tracking, printing, virtual hall players and MMPM/2 sound support.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-CARDS-DICE-Bingo2/blob/549ebbf59ca10ea1a77f47cdbca94146af800dc8/README.md
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-CARDS-DICE-Bingo2/blob/549ebbf59ca10ea1a77f47cdbca94146af800dc8/src/BINGO.C

### Classification (reviewed)

Includes BINGO.EXE and BINGOWAV.DLL source/resources; supports up to 12 player cards and 99 virtual hall players according to README. Material inconsistency: README says GPLv3, but doc/LICENSE and legacy/LICENSE both contain GPLv2. Record as mixed/inconsistent license evidence rather than asserting GPLv3.

### Identity (reviewed)

Stangl Roman’s 1992 ball-rolling OS/2 PM game, including its 2017 anniversary-era source and a 2026 Open Watcom continuation. The draw thread manages the playfield, weighted random symbols, deflectors, holes, movement and points while the UI accepts mouse input and maintains settings.

### Identity (reviewed)

Kent Lundberg’s 1993–2002 native OS/2 Presentation Manager version of robots, continued for ArcaOS. GAME.C implements robot pursuit, collisions forming heaps, legal moves, teleport, sonic screwdriver, scoring and level progression; separate source covers PM UI and high scores.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-PUZZLE-PM_Robots/blob/0515fdca1e31dc8e7da1dc35f1ef40aaf5a0634a/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Michael G. Slack’s OS/2 Sibyl/Object Pascal cribbage game source archive, with an engine tracing back through a DOS Turbo Pascal port to David Addison’s Amiga A-basic game. The recovered OS/2 engine scores hands and play, recommends discards and chooses computer cards; visual forms, configuration and resources are included.
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-CARDS-Cribbage/blob/5a173736b41fd95ab1d75b6aa29a76649a2ca884/README.md
- https://github.com/OS2World/GAME-CARDS-Cribbage/blob/5a173736b41fd95ab1d75b6aa29a76649a2ca884/CribEngine.pas

Actual Sibyl project and form/resource files are present, but QCardU/QCard2 is an external dependency. README explicitly says supplied Windows resources were largely untested; no standalone build claim is made. README and included LICENSE specify GPLv2.

### Identity (reviewed)

New native PC-98 MS-DOS adventure engine by PokuG/Yarufu. It interprets Shift-JIS story scripts with branching, choices, flags and save/load, drives PC-98 planar graphics and GRCG output, and optionally uses PMD BGM and DOS mouse input. This is native homebrew, distinct from recovered ADV98V commercial-engine research.
- https://github.com/yarufu/pc98/blob/77db438ef661b484df3e7c1ba98eb44ae4644c75/README.md
- https://github.com/yarufu/pc98/blob/77db438ef661b484df3e7c1ba98eb44ae4644c75/src/script.c
- https://github.com/yarufu/pc98/blob/77db438ef661b484df3e7c1ba98eb44ae4644c75/src/graph98.c

- https://github.com/yarufu/pc98/commit/77db438ef661b484df3e7c1ba98eb44ae4644c75

### Classification (reviewed)

Substantial native graphics and script implementation; source review confirmed command parsing and direct GRCG planar output. Author describes an experimental evolving engine. Includes script specification, design/save/performance notes and sample scripts. MIT License (2026 PokuG stdio.h) included.

### Identity (reviewed)

Independent preservation repository containing transcriptions and tape-derived source for Burroughs B5500 software, including University of Washington APL, Wirth EULER, Heriot-Watt Pascal, SNOBOL, Lisp, system patches and CUBE user-library tapes. Substantial recovered implementations and per-program provenance distinguish this from an emulator-only record.
- https://github.com/retro-software/B5500-software/blob/0de98b96419bd2f97dabecbf812ada60c50364d5/README.md
- https://github.com/retro-software/B5500-software/blob/0de98b96419bd2f97dabecbf812ada60c50364d5/LICENSE.txt
- https://github.com/retro-software/B5500-software/blob/0de98b96419bd2f97dabecbf812ada60c50364d5/APL-UW-Kildall/APL-IMAGE.alg_m
- https://github.com/retro-software/B5500-software/blob/0de98b96419bd2f97dabecbf812ada60c50364d5/APL-UW-Kildall/README.txt
- https://github.com/retro-software/B5500-software/blob/0de98b96419bd2f97dabecbf812ada60c50364d5/PASCAL-Heriot-Watt/SYMBOL.PASCAL.alg_m
- https://github.com/retro-software/B5500-software/blob/0de98b96419bd2f97dabecbf812ada60c50364d5/PASCAL-Heriot-Watt/README.txt
- https://github.com/retro-software/B5500-software/blob/0de98b96419bd2f97dabecbf812ada60c50364d5/EULER-Wirth/EULERIV.alg_m
- https://github.com/retro-software/B5500-software/commit/0de98b96419bd2f97dabecbf812ada60c50364d5

### Classification (reviewed)

APL transcription was corrected against recovered CUBE tapes; sequence numbers/card columns are significant. Later Pascal patched runtime changes are missing. Collection is intentionally one record, not one record per directory. Root MIT licence covers preservation contributions, but original embedded notices remain significant: APL explicitly prohibits sale/lease and requires named implementor credit. Do not characterize entire historic collection as uniformly MIT.
- https://github.com/retro-software/B5500-software/blob/0de98b96419bd2f97dabecbf812ada60c50364d5/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Howard Bussey preserves the SDS-940 BASIC implementation he extended at NOAA around 1970 with formatted output, multiple file I/O and strings. Source returned through the Hawaii BCC-500 lineage contains the compiler, runtime and scratch-page sections rather than a modern BASIC clone.
- https://github.com/at7heb/940Basic/blob/1001c4abfc255f34895951385d3ccfb8fcc842b9/README.md
- https://github.com/at7heb/940Basic/blob/1001c4abfc255f34895951385d3ccfb8fcc842b9/LICENSE

### Classification (reviewed)

S6B header identifies 1973 BASIC compiler and scanner/intermediate-to-machine-code stages; source uses POP/SYSPOP runtime operations. README explains provenance and page mapping. MIT, copyright 2024 Howard Bussey; historical provenance explicitly documented by original contributor.

### Identity (reviewed)

Recovered original CMS assembler source, macro definitions and assembly listings from old CP/67-format tapes. Recovery used VM/370 and z/VM conversion before ASCII extraction. The repository name mentions CP/67; inspected payload is specifically CMS, with source headers dated 1973 as well as older material, not a verified complete CP kernel.
- https://github.com/moshix/CP-67-CMS-Source/blob/d02f67e34987e1c95deea734ea456dc9dbe2223a/README.md
- https://github.com/moshix/CP-67-CMS-Source/blob/d02f67e34987e1c95deea734ea456dc9dbe2223a/CMSSource.txt

- https://github.com/moshix/CP-67-CMS-Source/commit/d02f67e34987e1c95deea734ea456dc9dbe2223a

### Classification (reviewed)

Primary source includes CMS command dispatch, LOAD/LOADMOD and disk-resident facilities. Two tape files had permanent read errors; README warns content may be lost. Avoid conflating repository 1969 description with 1973 dated source headers. No repository licence file or explicit reuse grant found in reviewed files; preserved IBM historical material has unclear redistribution terms.

### Identity (reviewed)

Pascal Bourguignon reconstructs the 1961 IBM 7090 LISP 1.5 punched-card source from Bonnie’s Birthday Assembly listing, preserving card layout and known typographical quirks while generating some tables and inferring five obscured cards. Includes original listing PDF, reconstructed assembly, generator Lisp, assembler patches and run recipe.
- https://github.com/informatimago/lisp-1-5/blob/fddd37ed99ed7808825455dfc9e74cb0d36eae6b/README
- https://github.com/informatimago/lisp-1-5/blob/fddd37ed99ed7808825455dfc9e74cb0d36eae6b/lisp15.asm
- https://github.com/informatimago/lisp-1-5/blob/fddd37ed99ed7808825455dfc9e74cb0d36eae6b/lisp15.lisp

- https://github.com/informatimago/lisp-1-5/commit/fddd37ed99ed7808825455dfc9e74cb0d36eae6b

### Classification (reviewed)

Explicitly not bit-perfect: generated property lists may preserve structure but differ in bits; original ID columns missing; five cards inferred and assembler differences documented. No root licence; Makefile and modern support-code headers explicitly AGPLv3-or-later. Original historical Lisp deck rights are not thereby established.
- https://github.com/informatimago/lisp-1-5/blob/fddd37ed99ed7808825455dfc9e74cb0d36eae6b/README

### Identity (reviewed)

C++/Qt tool decodes the Tioga rich-text file format used for most Xerox Cedar/Mesa archived sources, displays documentation and syntax-highlighted source, and supplies a Cedar lexer plus experimental parser. Derived partly from archived Andrew/Emacs decoder plugins to make the 1993 Solaris Cedar tree inspectable.
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/blob/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a/Readme.md
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/blob/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a/TiogaReader.cpp
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/blob/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a/CedarLexer.cpp
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/blob/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a/CedarParser.cpp
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/blob/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a/TiogaViewer.pro
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/blob/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a/LICENSE.LGPLv21
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/blob/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a/LICENSE.LGPLv3
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/blob/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a/LICENSE.GPLv3
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/commit/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a

### Classification (reviewed)

Decoder implements Tioga nodes/control opcodes. Parser and cross-referencer are work in progress; author reports grammar incompatibilities and reluctance to continue. Viewer remains usable as source-analysis tooling. TiogaReader.cpp explicitly LGPL 2.1 or 3 with 1993 Xerox and 2023 Keller copyrights; repository includes LGPL 2.1/3 and GPLv3 texts, so inspect component headers rather than assume one uniform licence.
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/blob/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a/Readme.md
- https://github.com/rochus-keller/Cedar/blob/08a5bae54e8a4c121586df936247ee20d8dd1d6a/TiogaReader.cpp

### Identity (reviewed)

Reimplements the asterisk-squasher coroutine example from Melvin Conway’s 1963 compiler paper in modern x86/x86-64 assembly, with Linux, macOS and Windows versions, both subroutine and coroutine styles and smoke-test fixtures. Focused historical programming-technique reconstruction rather than a full compiler.
- https://github.com/codecop/Conways-Squasher-Coroutine/blob/87204dbc0d17635fd7fc895052308c98a3767b7b/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Elixir implementation of an SDS-940 assembler with opcode map, lexical and expression parsing, symbol resolution, pseudo-operations, relocatable address handling and assembly listing output. README is boilerplate, but substantive assembler implementation and tests establish its purpose.
- https://github.com/at7heb/a940/blob/73e8177c797980499528cba463e8f2745af9d94c/LICENSE

### Identity (reviewed)

Elixir SDS-940 user-mode simulator with CPU execution, memory mapping, process/machine state and host-side SYSPOP implementation. Phoenix umbrella provides surrounding application infrastructure. Scope is running many user programs rather than reconstructing a complete original TSS kernel.
- https://github.com/at7heb/simTSS/blob/fa562de6f665652590d33acd0d5667a87e69e3bd/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Neil Higgins’s reusable Python LGP-30 simulator models instructions, drum memory, Flexowriter input/output, paper-tape and Intel HEX loading, with interactive debugging controls and recovered program examples. Its stated goal is a hackable simulator suited to future MicroPython use rather than cycle accuracy.
- https://github.com/etherbrat/sim_lgp30/blob/3b7a546a699bcf3c262d464dc5a063416efc6a22/README.MD
- https://github.com/etherbrat/sim_lgp30/blob/3b7a546a699bcf3c262d464dc5a063416efc6a22/src/sim_lgp30/lgp30_cpu.py
- https://github.com/etherbrat/sim_lgp30/blob/3b7a546a699bcf3c262d464dc5a063416efc6a22/src/sim_lgp30/lgp30_drum.py

- https://github.com/etherbrat/sim_lgp30/commit/3b7a546a699bcf3c262d464dc5a063416efc6a22

### Classification (reviewed)

Manual reports successful tape/bootstrap and Blackjack execution, but not independently verified. Utility-first; cycle accuracy explicitly not a goal. Manual states current nonblocking console is Windows 11 dependent and needs changes for Linux. No standard root licence. PDF manual says code/manual are free to use as desired with acceptance of consequences; permissive informal wording, no SPDX licence asserted.

### Identity (reviewed)

Neil Higgins’s two-pass LGP-30 cross-assembler provides structural validation, symbols and expressions, directives, formatted listings and Intel HEX binary output. Modernized syntax intentionally differs from the historical native assemblers.
- https://github.com/etherbrat/xasm_lgp30/blob/d10cccaec57a527f9b2af8ac536182b5029693d7/README.md
- https://github.com/etherbrat/xasm_lgp30/blob/d10cccaec57a527f9b2af8ac536182b5029693d7/src/xasm_lgp30/assembler.py
- https://github.com/etherbrat/xasm_lgp30/blob/d10cccaec57a527f9b2af8ac536182b5029693d7/src/xasm_lgp30/expression_parserV4.py
- https://github.com/etherbrat/xasm_lgp30/blob/d10cccaec57a527f9b2af8ac536182b5029693d7/src/xasm_lgp30/assembler_constants.py

- https://github.com/etherbrat/xasm_lgp30/blob/d10cccaec57a527f9b2af8ac536182b5029693d7/docs/xasm-lgp30-manual.pdf
- https://github.com/etherbrat/xasm_lgp30/commit/d10cccaec57a527f9b2af8ac536182b5029693d7

### Classification (reviewed)

Implementation validates first pass, resolves/emits second pass and supports expression parser. Source TODO warns it can assemble past physical memory limit. No standard root licence; manual gives informal free-to-use wording with responsibility disclaimer. No SPDX licence inferred.

### Identity (reviewed)

Companion Python tool converts sparse Intel HEX LGP-30 binaries into v-formatted LGP-30 paper-tape text, preserving start address in Stop and Transfer records. Implementation groups segments into chunks, writes tape headers and encodes the machine’s nonstandard hexadecimal alphabet.
- https://github.com/etherbrat/hex2tape_lgp30/blob/56333f57ce2e299b945de01383a17de2d5e34437/src/hex2tape_lgp30/hex2tape.py

### Identity (reviewed)

Reusable C11 LGP-30 core with drum rotational timing, all instruction implementations, Flexowriter/simplified I/O interfaces, integrated assembler and memory-image format, plus batch CLI and interactive debugger. Independent implementation has tests and detailed manual-linked instruction semantics.
- https://github.com/dennis-panchenko/lgp-30-emulator/blob/fbf49a603b5d025c4dd6a26968cf38b6534b06dc/README.md

### Classification (reviewed)

Step-driven core has no global machine state and uses I/O callbacks; REPL exposes registers, stepping, breakpoints and reset. Documentation accuracy claims are author assertions pending runtime verification. MIT, copyright Dennis Panchenko 2026.

### Identity (reviewed)

Compact independent Go assembler for LGP-30 source with labels, opcode/order table, literal packing, multiple number notations, two-pass symbol replacement and native-word emission. Unit tests exercise literals and parsing; implemented tool distinct from the author’s browser-emulator code.
- https://github.com/WizardOfHaas/LGP-ASM/blob/f580a52436c7048b0620cc4a8027d652c2a1c5f8/main.go
